## 2.0.0 — 2026-08-09

Major architecture release.

### Agent
- Retains the 1.1.5 read-only driver core and threat-intelligence correlation.
- AV/EDR inventory from SecurityCenter2, services, processes, installed applications, Defender status and driver fingerprints.
- CiTool/App Control policy inventory with fallback to active CIP files.
- PnP/INF/device provenance.
- Explainable Threat Intelligence, Exposure, Active Risk and Remediation scores.
- Persistent Agent ID, Site and Tags.
- First Seen / Last Seen history and driver change detection.
- Protection regression and security-product change detection.
- Driver event timeline.
- SHA-256-addressed persistent PE-analysis cache.
- Bounded PowerShell 7 parallel static pre-analysis with serial fallback.
- Structured JSONL logging and stable exit codes.
- Local HTML/JSON/CSV/Wazuh output.
- Offline collector spool and retry.
- Scheduled-task installer/uninstaller.

### Collector
- FastAPI + SQLite/WAL collector.
- API-key authenticated ingest.
- Idempotent uploads by Agent ID + Scan ID.
- Gzip-compressed raw snapshots plus normalized findings/changes/inventory.
- Central dashboard and per-host history.
- Fleet driver search by file name or SHA-256.
- Retention controls.
- Central Wazuh NDJSON output.
- Docker deployment with non-root user, dropped capabilities and health check.

### Wazuh
- v2 JSON rules for findings, loaded known-vulnerable drivers, driver hash changes, protection regression and security-product disappearance.

### Safety
- No exploit execution, IOCTL exploitation, EDR/AV termination, PPL manipulation, driver loading/unloading or security-control modification.

`Docs/API.md`:

```md
# Collector API

Basis: `https://<collector>/api/v1`

## Health

```http
GET /api/v1/health
```

Keine Authentisierung.

## Ingest

```http
POST /api/v1/ingest
X-DRS-API-Key: <ingest-secret>
Content-Type: application/json
```

Body Schema 2.0:

```json
{
  "schema_version": "2.0",
  "report": {
    "scan_id": "...",
    "created_at": "...",
    "tool": {},
    "host": {},
    "statistics": {},
    "protection": {},
    "app_control": {},
    "security_products": {},
    "findings": [],
    "changes": {}
  },
  "wazuh_events": [],
  "inventory": [],
  "provenance": []
}
```

Antwort:

```json
{
  "accepted": true,
  "host_id": "...",
  "snapshot_id": 123,
  "sha256": "..."
}
```

## Admin API

HTTP Basic Auth:

```text
GET /api/v1/hosts
GET /api/v1/hosts/{host_id}
GET /api/v1/snapshots/{snapshot_id}
```

## Speicherung

Raw Payload:
- SHA-256 über den empfangenen JSON-Body,
- gzip-komprimiert in SQLite,
- Findings/Changes zusätzlich normalisiert für Dashboard-Abfragen.


## Fleet Driver Search

```text
GET /api/v1/drivers?name=IOMap64.sys
GET /api/v1/drivers?sha256=<64-hex>
GET /api/v1/drivers?host_id=<id>
```

Das Dashboard bietet dieselbe Suche unter `/drivers`.


## Finding Investigation API

```text
GET /api/v1/findings/{finding_id}
GET /api/v1/findings/{finding_id}?refresh=true
```

Returns:

- normalized finding metadata,
- full evidence object sent by the Agent,
- optional cached/live GitHub and NVD enrichment.

HTML Investigation page:

```text
GET /finding/{finding_id}
GET /finding/{finding_id}?refresh=true
```

`refresh=true` bypasses the normal online-enrichment cache for that investigation.

The API never returns the configured GitHub/NVD API secrets.


## Exploit Intelligence fields

Finding JSON may include:

```text
ExploitIntelMatchCount
ExploitIntelMatches
ExploitIntelCves
ExploitIntelReferences
ExploitIntelTypes
ExploitIntelMaxCVSS
HasPublicPoCReference
ResearchMaturityScore
```

The latest report also includes:

```text
report.exploit_intel.inventory_leads
```

The Collector host HTML page renders those inventory leads from the latest stored snapshot.

`Docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`:

```md
# Architektur

## Zielbild

```text
┌──────────────────────── Windows Endpoint ─────────────────────────┐
│ DriverRiskScout Agent                                             │
│                                                                   │
│ Core Driver Scan ─┐                                               │
│ AV/EDR Inventory ─┤                                               │
│ App Control/CiTool├─> Risk/Change Engine -> Local Reports         │
│ PnP Provenance ───┤                    │                          │
│ Event Timeline ───┘                    ├─> Wazuh NDJSON           │
│                                       └─> HTTPS Collector Upload  │
│                                              │                    │
│                   Offline failure -> Spool <-┘                    │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                               │
                                               v
┌──────────────────── DriverRiskScout Collector ────────────────────┐
│ FastAPI API                                                      │
│  POST /api/v1/ingest  (X-DRS-API-Key)                            │
│  Read-only Dashboard (HTTP Basic / reverse proxy)                │
│                                                                  │
│ SQLite WAL                                                       │
│  Hosts | Snapshots | Findings | Changes                          │
│                                                                  │
│ Central Wazuh NDJSON -> optional Wazuh Manager                   │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## Trust Model

Der Collector erhält **keine administrativen Zugangsdaten** für Endpoints und startet keine Remote-Prozesse. Endpoints führen den Scanner lokal aus und senden ausschließlich Messergebnisse an den Collector.

Vorteile:
- kein Domain-Admin-/Local-Admin-Schlüssel auf dem Collector,
- kein Remote-Service-Control- oder WinRM-Zwang,
- Firewall-Regel nur Client -> Collector notwendig,
- Agent funktioniert bei Collector-Ausfall weiter,
- fehlgeschlagene Uploads werden lokal gespult.

## Agent-Pipeline

1. Konfiguration laden.
2. Pending-Spool erneut senden.
3. Driver-Core starten.
4. LOLDrivers / Microsoft Blocklist / BYOVD Profiles korrelieren.
5. AV/EDR inventarisieren.
6. CiTool/App Control inventarisieren.
7. PnP/INF-Provenance korrelieren.
8. Timeline normalisieren.
9. Explainable Risk berechnen.
10. First/Last Seen und Regressionen bestimmen.
11. HTML/JSON/CSV/Wazuh erzeugen.
12. Optional vollständiges Inventar an Collector senden.
13. Exitcode nach höchster Priorität setzen.

## Datenspeicher

Endpoint:
- Reports: `C:\ProgramData\DriverRiskScout\Reports`
- State: `C:\ProgramData\DriverRiskScout\State`
- PE cache: `C:\ProgramData\DriverRiskScout\Cache`
- Offline spool: `C:\ProgramData\DriverRiskScout\Spool`
- Wazuh: `C:\ProgramData\DriverRiskScout\wazuh-driver-findings.ndjson`

Collector:
- SQLite: `/data/drs.sqlite3`
- Wazuh: `/data/wazuh/driver-risk-scout.ndjson`

## Cache-Sicherheitsmodell

Der PE-Cache wird **nach einem frisch berechneten SHA-256** adressiert. DriverRiskScout vertraut nicht nur auf `LastWriteTime` oder Dateigröße, weil ein Angreifer Zeitstempel manipulieren kann. Somit kann eine geänderte Datei den statischen Analysecache nicht allein durch gleichbleibende Metadaten wiederverwenden.

## Skalierung

SQLite + WAL ist für kleine/mittlere interne Installationen und typische tägliche Agent-Scans geeignet. Für große Flotten sollte der Collector später auf PostgreSQL, einen Queue-Layer und mehrere API-Worker erweitert werden. Das Agent-Protokoll (`/api/v1/ingest`, Schema 2.0) bleibt dabei stabil.

```

`Docs/CONFIGURATION.md`:

```md
# Konfiguration

Datei: `Agent/config/config.json`

Die Beispielkonfiguration liegt in `config.example.json`.

## Scan

```json
"Scan": {
  "IncludeStopped": true,
  "DriverStore": true,
  "EventLogs": true,
  "LookbackDays": 30,
  "EnableNvdLookup": false,
  "EnableYara": false,
  "YaraExecutable": "",
  "EnableDeviceAclProbe": false,
  "MaxDeviceAclProbes": 25,
  "EnableOnlineCertificateRevocation": false
}
```

### Empfehlungen

Produktive Clients:
- `IncludeStopped=true`
- `DriverStore=true`
- `EventLogs=true`
- `EnableDeviceAclProbe=false`
- `EnableNvdLookup=false` wenn Internetzugriffe minimiert werden sollen
- YARA nur aktivieren, wenn `yara.exe` kontrolliert verteilt wird

Lab/Audit:
- Device ACL Probe optional
- Online Certificate Revocation optional
- NVD/YARA optional

## Collector

```json
"Collector": {
  "Enabled": true,
  "Url": "https://drs.example.local",
  "ApiKeyEnvironmentVariable": "DRS_COLLECTOR_API_KEY",
  "SkipCertificateCheck": false,
  "TimeoutSeconds": 30,
  "RetrySpoolBeforeScan": true
}
```

`SkipCertificateCheck=true` nur kurzfristig im Lab. Nicht als Dauerlösung verwenden.

## Full Inventory

```json
"IncludeFullInventoryInAgentPayload": true
```

Bei `true` erhält der Collector das vollständige `.sys`-Inventar plus Provenance. Bei sehr großen Flotten kann dies deaktiviert werden, wenn nur Findings/State benötigt werden.

## Exitcodes

- 0: erfolgreich, kein Medium/High/Critical
- 10: mindestens Medium
- 20: mindestens High
- 30: mindestens Critical
- 50: für partielle Scans reserviert
- 90: interner Fehler

Die Werte sind konfigurierbar.

## Wazuh

```json
"Wazuh": {
  "Enabled": true,
  "OutputPath": "C:\\ProgramData\\DriverRiskScout\\wazuh-driver-findings.ndjson"
}
```


## First-run Change Suppression

```json
"State": {
  "SuppressInitialChangeEvents": true
}
```

Bei `true` wird der erste Lauf als Baseline angelegt, ohne für jede bereits vorhandene Treiberdatei `driver_new` und für jedes aktive Security-Produkt `security_product_added` zu erzeugen. Ab dem zweiten Lauf werden Änderungen normal gemeldet.


## Identity / Site / Tags

```json
"Identity": {
  "Site": "Kunde-A-Dortmund",
  "Tags": ["workstation", "finance"]
}
```

Beim ersten Lauf wird eine persistente Agent-ID unter `C:\\ProgramData\\DriverRiskScout\\State\\agent-id.txt` erzeugt. Sie bleibt bei einer Hostnamenänderung gleich. `Site` und `Tags` werden zentral an den Collector übertragen und dienen der organisatorischen Zuordnung; sie sind keine Sicherheits-/Mandantentrennung.


## Investigation detail pages

Agent configuration:

```json
"Investigation": {
  "GenerateDetailPages": true,
  "IncludeGitHubSearchLinks": true,
  "IncludeSafeValidationCommands": true
}
```

`GenerateDetailPages=true` creates a static read-only evidence page for every prioritized driver finding.

Live GitHub/NVD API enrichment is a **Collector** feature and is configured through the Collector environment, not the endpoint Agent.


## Exploit Intelligence

```json
"Scan": {
  "EnableExploitIntel": true,
  "EnableExploitIntelOnline": false,
  "ExploitDbPath": "profiles\\exploit-db.json",
  "GenerateSigmaRules": true
}
```

`config.imported-v2.3.0.json` contains a migrated version of the supplied configuration. It preserves the supplied local report/state paths, scan toggles and compatible exit codes while retaining DriverRiskScout's environment-variable based Collector API-key handling.

```

`Docs/EXPLOIT-INTELLIGENCE.md`:

```md
# Exploit Intelligence 2.3

DriverRiskScout 2.3 integrates the supplied `exploit-db.json` as a **research/evidence database**.

## Supplied source provenance

- exploit-db.json SHA256: `bcf5110dfa670b053f5c38b211f84747df5dcddd172b26dbd0db8985116ca59d`
- config-v2.3.0.json SHA256: `fd194103429cc14a5408924dd9bb4b81c753fdfb908ad1a6e062bd3ac8fb2576`
- DriverRiskScout-v2.3.0.ps1 SHA256: `7f9c01bc58065f575bce6d595849b3c75b789f4a0d642d2ec44474549c9b9a60`

The database is copied into:

```text
Agent/profiles/exploit-db.json
```

It is parsed locally and is never executed.

## Important applicability rule

An `AffectedDrivers` filename mapping means:

> this research record mentions this driver filename.

It does **not** mean:

> every current file with this filename is vulnerable.

The supplied database does not contain affected version/build ranges. Therefore DriverRiskScout records:

```text
Applicability:
Affected driver filename mapping only;
affected version/build is not provided by this database
```

Exact local SHA256 evidence from LOLDrivers/Microsoft/other exact-hash sources remains stronger evidence.

## New finding fields

```text
ExploitIntelMatchCount
ExploitIntelMatches
ExploitIntelCves
ExploitIntelReferences
ExploitIntelTypes
ExploitIntelMaxCVSS
HasPublicPoCReference
ExploitIntelSourceVerifiedFlag
ResearchMaturityScore
ExploitIntelSummary
```

### ResearchMaturityScore

`ResearchMaturityScore` is intentionally separate from:

- CurrentExploitabilityScore
- PotentialIfLoadedScore
- PrivilegeEscalationScore

It measures how much structured public/research evidence is available, not whether exploitation succeeds on the endpoint.

The supplied `Verified` field is exposed as:

```text
SourceVerifiedFlag
```

It means the **source database marks the record verified**. DriverRiskScout does not claim independent verification.

## Online enrichment

Agent setting:

```json
"EnableExploitIntelOnline": true
```

performs NVD enrichment for CVEs already associated with a finding. It does not keyword-search arbitrary drivers and does not download PoCs.

The central Collector can additionally query public GitHub/NVD data when its online enrichment is enabled.

## GitHub / Metasploit references

The Collector searches public GitHub research by:

- exact SHA256,
- filename,
- CVE,
- GitHub Advisory Database,
- and, when a GitHub token is configured, code references in `rapid7/metasploit-framework`.

These are links/metadata only.

DriverRiskScout does not clone, compile, import or execute repository code.

## Sigma

`GenerateSigmaRules=true` creates experimental Sysmon Event ID 6 driver-load detection rules in:

```text
<report directory>/sigma-rules/
```

For a valid SHA256 the rule includes both:

- driver filename
- exact SHA256

This is defensive detection content only.

```

`Docs/EXPLOITABILITY-SCORING.md`:

```md
# Exploitability, Reachability und Privilege-Escalation Score

DriverRiskScout 2.1 trennt bewusst drei Fragen:

1. Ist ein Treiber nach Threat Intelligence verwundbar?
2. Ist seine Angriffsfläche auf diesem Endpoint aktuell erreichbar?
3. Gibt es Evidenz für einen Primitive, der Privilege Escalation oder Kernel-Kompromittierung ermöglichen könnte?

## Current Exploitability Score (0–100)

Der Hauptscore priorisiert die manuelle Prüfung des aktuellen Endpoint-Zustands.

| Score | Level | Interpretation |
|---:|---|---|
| 0–29 | Low | aktuelle Ausnutzbarkeit nicht belegt / geringe aktuelle Exposure |
| 30–49 | Guarded | relevante Evidenz, aber wichtige Glieder der Angriffskette fehlen |
| 50–69 | Elevated | mehrere relevante Faktoren; manuelle Analyse priorisieren |
| 70–84 | High | starke aktuelle Exposure-Evidenz |
| 85–100 | Critical | sehr starke Kombination aus Vulnerability, Aktivität, Reachability und Primitive |

Der Score ist **keine Exploit-Wahrscheinlichkeit**. 90/100 bedeutet nicht „90 % Erfolgswahrscheinlichkeit“.

## Potential If Loaded Score

Ein bekannter verwundbarer Treiber kann derzeit gestoppt sein. Deshalb wird zusätzlich berechnet, wie die Priorität aussehen würde, wenn der Treiber geladen wäre.

Eine permissive ACL wird dabei nicht angenommen. Sie zählt nur, wenn sie beobachtet wurde.

## Vulnerability Confidence

- Very High – exakte Known-Evidenz + Verified-Metadaten
- High – exakte Known-/Blocklist-/Profile-Evidenz
- Medium – Versions-/Dateinamen-Hinweis
- Low-Medium – Heuristik
- Low – keine exakte öffentliche Vulnerability-Evidenz

## Reachability

- Not currently active
- Broad device access indicated
- Device present
- Static device reference only
- Loaded without observed user-mode interface

`BroadDeviceAcl=false` ist **kein Beweis**, dass normale Benutzer keinen Zugriff besitzen. Ohne erfolgreichen ACL-Probe bleibt Low-Privilege-Reachability unbekannt.

## Primitive / Privilege Impact

Primitive werden nur als belastbarer behandelt, wenn Threat Intelligence oder ein kuratiertes Profil eine entsprechende Klasse liefert.

Beispiele:
- Privilege escalation
- Kernel/physical memory access
- Code-integrity bypass
- Security-control bypass/tamper
- Process termination

Statische Imports und IOCTL-Nummern allein beweisen keine ausnutzbare Schwachstelle.

## Privilege Escalation Score

Ein zweiter 0–100-Score priorisiert speziell lokale Rechteausweitung. Er berücksichtigt:
- bekannte Vulnerability-Evidenz,
- Primitive-Klasse,
- Running/Recent Load,
- Device-Präsenz,
- optional beobachtete breite ACL.

Mögliche Aussagen:
- Threat intelligence indicates a privilege-escalation primitive
- Potential privilege escalation via known kernel/physical-memory primitive
- Heuristic only - privilege escalation not established
- Known vulnerable sample, but privilege-escalation primitive is not established by current metadata
- Not demonstrated by current evidence

## Read-only-Grenze

DriverRiskScout lädt keine verwundbaren Treiber, sendet keine IOCTLs und versucht keine Rechteausweitung. Der Score basiert auf Evidenz und Exposure, nicht auf invasivem Exploit-Testing.

## Tuning

Gewichte und Schwellenwerte liegen in:

`Agent/profiles/exploitability-policy.json`

```

`Docs/INSTALLATION-AGENT.md`:

```md
# Installation – Windows Agent

## Voraussetzungen

- Windows 10/11 oder unterstütztes Windows Server.
- PowerShell 7.x (`pwsh.exe`).
- Administratorrechte empfohlen; für vollständige Treiber-/Security-Abfragen und die Scheduled-Task-Installation erforderlich.
- HTTPS-Verbindung zum Collector, falls Agent-Modus genutzt wird.

## Manuell

```powershell
cd C:\Tools\DriverRiskScout-2.0.0\Agent
Copy-Item .\config\config.example.json .\config\config.json
.\Test-DriverRiskScout.ps1
.\DriverRiskScout.ps1 -ConfigPath .\config\config.json -Mode Local
```

## Dauerinstallation

Erhöhte PowerShell 7:

```powershell
.\Install-Agent.ps1 `
  -CollectorUrl 'https://drs.example.local' `
  -ApiKey '<random-api-key>' `
  -ScheduleTime '03:15'
```

Standard:
- Programmdateien: `C:\Program Files\DriverRiskScout`
- Daten: `C:\ProgramData\DriverRiskScout`
- Scheduled Task: `DriverRiskScout Daily`
- Task läuft als `SYSTEM`.

Das API-Key-Geheimnis wird bei Verwendung von `-ApiKey` als Machine Environment Variable `DRS_COLLECTOR_API_KEY` gespeichert. Alternativ kann es über RMM/GPO/Secrets-Management gesetzt werden.

## Ohne zentralen Server

```powershell
.\Install-Agent.ps1 -NoScheduledTask
```

und in `config.json`:

```json
{
  "Mode": "Local",
  "Collector": { "Enabled": false }
}
```

## Deinstallation

Programm entfernen, lokale Scan-Historie behalten:

```powershell
.\Uninstall-Agent.ps1
```

Alles einschließlich ProgramData und API-Key entfernen:

```powershell
.\Uninstall-Agent.ps1 -RemoveData -RemoveApiKey
```

## Device ACL Probe

Standardmäßig AUS:

```json
"EnableDeviceAclProbe": false
```

Nur kontrolliert in Test-/Audit-Umgebungen aktivieren. Der Probe öffnet einen `READ_CONTROL`-Handle, sendet aber keinen IOCTL.

```

`Docs/INSTALLATION-SERVER.md`:

```md
# Installation – zentraler Collector

## Empfohlene Variante: Docker

```bash
cp .env.example .env
```

Zwei verschiedene, lange Zufallswerte setzen:

```env
DRS_INGEST_API_KEY=...
DRS_ADMIN_USER=admin
DRS_ADMIN_PASSWORD=...
```

Danach:

```bash
docker compose up -d --build
docker compose logs -f
```

Health Check:

```bash
curl http://127.0.0.1:8088/api/v1/health
```

## Warum Port 8088 nur localhost?

`docker-compose.yml` veröffentlicht:

```text
127.0.0.1:8088:8088
```

Damit wird der Collector nicht versehentlich unverschlüsselt im LAN veröffentlicht. Für Clients muss ein TLS-Reverse-Proxy davor stehen.

Beispiel-Ziel:

```text
https://drs.example.local
        |
        v
Reverse Proxy TLS
        |
        v
127.0.0.1:8088
```

## Reverse Proxy

Jeder übliche HTTPS-Reverse-Proxy ist geeignet. Anforderungen:

- TLS 1.2/1.3,
- Body-Größe mindestens `DRS_MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES`,
- Forwarding an `http://127.0.0.1:8088`,
- optional zusätzliche IP-Allowlist / SSO / MFA für das Dashboard,
- `/api/v1/ingest` muss den Header `X-DRS-API-Key` unverändert weiterreichen.

## Daten sichern

Persistente Daten liegen im Docker Named Volume:

```text
driver-riskscout_drs_data
```

Enthalten sind SQLite-Datenbank und zentrale Wazuh-NDJSON-Datei. Für ein konsistentes Backup den Collector kurz stoppen oder ein SQLite-kompatibles Backup-Verfahren nutzen.

Beispiel für ein Dateibackup bei gestopptem Collector:

```bash
docker compose stop driver-riskscout-collector
docker run --rm \
  -v driver-riskscout_drs_data:/data:ro \
  -v "$PWD":/backup \
  alpine sh -c 'cd /data && tar czf /backup/driver-riskscout-data.tgz .'
docker compose start driver-riskscout-collector
```

## Retention

```env
DRS_RETENTION_DAYS=90
```

Alte Snapshots werden bei neuen Ingests bereinigt. Host-Objekte bleiben bestehen.

## Dashboard

Browser:

```text
https://drs.example.local/
```

Authentisierung: HTTP Basic mit `DRS_ADMIN_USER` / `DRS_ADMIN_PASSWORD` oder zusätzliche Authentisierung am Reverse Proxy.

## API- und Dashboard-Geheimnisse trennen

`DRS_INGEST_API_KEY` darf **nicht** identisch mit dem Dashboard-Passwort sein.


## Investigation Console – GitHub/NVD

```env
DRS_ONLINE_ENRICHMENT=true
DRS_ENRICHMENT_TTL_HOURS=12
DRS_ONLINE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=8
DRS_GITHUB_TOKEN=
DRS_NVD_API_KEY=
```

Without a GitHub token, public repository/issue/advisory queries may still work but are more rate limited. DriverRiskScout skips exact GitHub code search when no token is configured.

Enrichment results are cached in SQLite.

```

`Docs/INVESTIGATION-CONSOLE.md`:

```md
# Vulnerability Investigation Console

DriverRiskScout 2.2 adds a dedicated investigation page for every prioritized driver finding.

## Purpose

The console answers five separate questions:

1. **What exactly matched?** Exact SHA-256, version hint, curated profile or heuristic?
2. **What does the endpoint show locally?** Running state, service, device reachability, policy protections and scores.
3. **What do independent sources say?** LOLDrivers, vendor advisories, Microsoft, CVE/NVD, CISA KEV and GitHub.
4. **Can the known attack path be relevant on this endpoint?** Current vs potential-if-loaded exposure and PrivEsc evidence.
5. **How can an administrator validate it safely?** Read-only commands only.

## Local report

In `DriverRiskScout-v2-report.html`, click the driver name. Detail pages are created below:

```text
<scan folder>\investigations\
```

Each page contains local file/hash/signature/vendor/version evidence, exact match confidence, exploitability/PrivEsc scores, reachability, source links, safe validation commands and remediation.

## Central Collector

On the host page, click a driver name. The central route is:

```text
/finding/<finding_id>
```

The central page can add optional live GitHub and NVD enrichment.

## GitHub integration

DriverRiskScout searches public GitHub research signals using:

- driver filename,
- exact SHA-256,
- associated CVE IDs.

It can display:

- repository search results,
- issue / pull-request search results,
- exact code-search results when `DRS_GITHUB_TOKEN` is configured,
- GitHub Advisory Database records for CVEs.

GitHub results are **secondary evidence**. A repository or PoC mention does not prove that the current endpoint is exploitable. DriverRiskScout does not clone repositories, compile PoCs or execute repository code.

### Configuration

Collector `.env`:

```env
DRS_ONLINE_ENRICHMENT=true
DRS_ENRICHMENT_TTL_HOURS=12
DRS_ONLINE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=8

# Optional but recommended:
DRS_GITHUB_TOKEN=

# Optional:
DRS_NVD_API_KEY=
```

Use the lowest practical GitHub permissions. Do not use a high-privilege administrative token merely for public research.

## NVD and CISA KEV

For associated CVEs the Collector queries the NVD CVE API and can display:

- CVSS,
- CWE,
- vulnerability description,
- publication/modification dates,
- CISA KEV status,
- KEV due date and required action where available.

CISA KEV is a strong prioritization signal for exploitation in the wild, but does not prove exploitation on the scanned endpoint.

## Evidence priority

Recommended interpretation:

1. Exact local SHA-256.
2. Primary vendor advisory / CVE record.
3. Microsoft blocklist or platform policy.
4. NVD/CISA enrichment.
5. LOLDrivers driver-specific intelligence.
6. GitHub research/advisory results.
7. Filename/version hints.
8. Static heuristics.

Absence from one source is never silently converted into a "safe" verdict.

## Safe validation commands

Generated commands include only read-only checks, for example:

```powershell
Test-Path -LiteralPath 'C:\Windows\System32\drivers\example.sys'
Get-FileHash -LiteralPath 'C:\Windows\System32\drivers\example.sys' -Algorithm SHA256
Get-AuthenticodeSignature -LiteralPath 'C:\Windows\System32\drivers\example.sys' | Format-List *
Get-CimInstance Win32_SystemDriver | Where-Object { $_.PathName -match 'example.sys' }
sc.exe query "ActualDiscoveredServiceName"
sc.exe qc "ActualDiscoveredServiceName"
```

The console intentionally does **not** generate:

- `sc.exe create`
- `sc.exe start`
- driver-loading instructions
- DeviceIoControl / IOCTL execution
- PoC compilation/execution
- AV/EDR disabling or bypass

## Privacy / external queries

When online enrichment is enabled, the Collector can send public technical identifiers such as driver filename, SHA-256 and CVE ID to GitHub and NVD APIs.

For strict data-egress environments:

```env
DRS_ONLINE_ENRICHMENT=false
```

All local evidence and static source links remain available.


## Bundled exploit intelligence

DriverRiskScout 2.3 displays supplied `exploit-db.json` records in a dedicated table:

- CVE
- research/exploit type
- CVSS
- description
- source-provided `Verified` flag
- direct reference URL
- applicability limitation

The source `Verified` flag is not reinterpreted as independent DriverRiskScout verification.

A driver filename match is not sufficient to declare vulnerability when the supplied record does not provide an affected version/build range.

```

`Docs/LAB-VALIDATION.md`:

```md
# Lab Validation Mode

DriverRiskScout 2.2.1 adds a non-invasive Lab Validation Mode.

## What it does

For each prioritized driver, the scanner correlates:

- current `Win32_SystemDriver` state,
- existing Code Integrity events,
- existing driver/service installation events,
- existing Sysmon driver-load events,
- HVCI state,
- Microsoft vulnerable-driver blocklist state,
- enforced App Control policy count,
- device/ACL/primitive evidence already collected by DriverRiskScout.

It then shows:

- `LabValidationReadinessScore`
- runtime state
- registered service state
- matching Code Integrity event count
- matching install/service event count
- matching Sysmon driver-load event count
- block evidence / confidence

## Safety boundary

This mode does not:

```text
sc.exe create
sc.exe start
DeviceIoControl
IOCTL execution
driver loading
PoC download/build/run
```

Those actions would turn DriverRiskScout from an assessment product into an active exploitation/privilege-escalation harness.

## Intended workflow

1. Scan the endpoint.
2. Open the driver's Investigation page.
3. Review exact vulnerability evidence and external research.
4. Review Current/Potential/PrivEsc scores.
5. Review Lab Validation telemetry.
6. If deeper reproduction is justified, perform it separately in a disposable, isolated lab under your own testing procedure.
7. Run DriverRiskScout again; it will correlate resulting Code Integrity / driver-load / service telemetry if Windows logged it.

This preserves a clear separation between the security assessment product and invasive exploit reproduction.

```

`Docs/MERGE-NOTES-2.3.0.md`:

```md
# 2.3.0 Import / Merge Notes

The supplied `DriverRiskScout-v2.3.0.ps1` was reviewed as a feature source, but it was not copied verbatim over the modular project.

Integrated concepts:

- local exploit intelligence database
- CVE/research reference correlation
- public PoC/reference presence
- optional NVD enrichment
- Sigma generation
- GitHub/Metasploit research references
- enhanced report counters

Implementation corrections:

1. Exploit Intelligence is implemented as `Modules/ExploitIntel.psm1`.
2. Sigma generation is implemented as `Modules/Sigma.psm1`.
3. Research availability does not directly inflate endpoint exploitability.
4. The source `Verified` flag is labeled source-provided rather than independent verification.
5. Filename-only affected-driver mappings never become a `Known vulnerable` classification by themselves.
6. Online lookups enrich CVEs already associated with a finding rather than declaring a vulnerability from a loose keyword hit.
7. No PoC download, build, import or execution path was added.

The supplied script contained a result-collection issue in its parallel Exploit Intel pipeline: output was emitted by the parallel block while the function later returned a separate `$results` variable that was never populated. The integrated module avoids that pattern.

```

`Docs/OPERATIONS.md`:

```md
# Betrieb / Operations

## Täglicher Ablauf

Empfohlen: einmal pro Tag als SYSTEM. Zusätzlich kann nach größeren Treiber-/Firmware-/Security-Agent-Updates manuell gescannt werden.

## Was im SOC relevant ist

Hohe Priorität:
1. Known vulnerable + Running.
2. Exact Microsoft blocklist + Load-Telemetrie.
3. Protection regression (HVCI/Blocklist/Tamper Protection aus).
4. Security product removed/inactive.
5. Driver hash changed.
6. Neuer unbekannter Treiber + Device/IOCTL/Kernel-Heuristik.

Niedrigere Priorität:
- reine DriverStore-Leichen,
- alte, aber nicht als verwundbar bekannte Versionen,
- Filename-only Hinweise ohne Hashbestätigung.

## Baseline / First Seen

Beim ersten Lauf entstehen viele `driver_new`-Ereignisse. Das ist erwartbar. Für produktive Wazuh-Alarmierung kann der erste Lauf als Baseline betrachtet werden.

Ab Lauf 2 werden Veränderungen aussagekräftiger:
- `driver_new`
- `driver_removed`
- `driver_hash_changed`
- `driver_state_changed`
- `protection_changed`
- `security_product_added`
- `security_product_removed`

## Collector-Spool

Wenn Upload fehlschlägt:

```text
C:\ProgramData\DriverRiskScout\Spool\pending-*.json
```

Beim nächsten Agentlauf wird die Queue zuerst erneut übertragen.

## Log

Jeder Scanordner enthält:

```text
DriverRiskScout.log.jsonl
```

Format: JSON Lines, Felder `timestamp`, `level`, `message`, optional `data`.

## Ergebnisdateien

Wichtige Dateien:
- `DriverRiskScout-v2-report.html`
- `report-v2.json`
- `prioritized-findings-v2.csv`
- `driver-inventory.csv`
- `driver-provenance.csv`
- `driver-timeline.csv`
- `driver-changes.csv`
- `protection-changes.csv`
- `security-product-changes.csv`
- `wazuh-v2.ndjson`

## Performance

Der statische PE-Cache ist SHA-256-basiert und persistent. Dadurch werden unveränderte Binärdateien nicht erneut vollständig statisch geparst. Der SHA-256 wird weiterhin frisch validiert.

Die Hash-/Signatur-/PE-Voranalyse kann unter PowerShell 7 mit `Performance.MaxParallelism` parallel laufen. Standard ist 4. Korrelation, Risk-Scoring und Report-Erzeugung bleiben seriell/deterministisch. Bei einem Fehler in der Parallelphase fällt der Core automatisch auf serielle Analyse zurück.

Server/API können parallel Client-Uploads verarbeiten.

```

`Docs/REPORTING.md`:

```md
# Reporting und Scores

## Vier Scores

### Threat Intelligence Score
Wie stark ist die externe/kuratierte Evidenz?
- exakter bekannter Vulnerable-/Blocked-Hash,
- Verified-Status,
- Microsoft Blocklist,
- exaktes BYOVD-Profil.

### Exposure Score
Wie erreichbar/interessant ist die lokale Angriffsfläche?
- System32-Treiber,
- Device Interface,
- breite Device ACL,
- IOCTL-Kandidaten,
- sensitive Kernel-Imports.

### Active Risk Score
Wie aktiv ist der Treiber?
- aktuell running,
- kürzlich geladen,
- kürzlich installiert,
- Schutzmaßnahmen werden mindernd berücksichtigt.

### Remediation Score
Gewichtete Priorität aus den drei Bereichen. Dient zur Reihenfolge der Bearbeitung, **nicht** zur Auswahl eines Exploits.

## Explainability

`WhyFlagged` enthält die einzelnen Score-Beiträge.

Beispiel:

```text
+70 known vulnerable/blocked intelligence match;
+15 source marked verified;
+20 present in System32 drivers;
-10 HVCI enabled;
-10 vulnerable-driver blocklist enabled
```

## Provenance

`driver-provenance.csv` korreliert Treiberdateien mit `Win32_PnPSignedDriver`:
- Device Name,
- Device Class,
- Provider,
- Manufacturer,
- INF,
- Device ID,
- aktuell gebunden ja/nein.

## Timeline

`driver-timeline.csv` normalisiert:
- Sysmon Driver Load Event 6,
- Code Integrity Events,
- Driver Service Installation Events.

## AV/EDR

Erkennung ist evidenzbasiert:
- SecurityCenter2,
- laufende Dienste,
- laufende Prozesse,
- installierte Anwendungen,
- Defender-spezifisch `Get-MpComputerStatus`.

Ein installierter Registry-Eintrag allein wird nicht automatisch als „Active“ gewertet.


## Exploit Intelligence 2.3 outputs

```text
exploit-intel.json
exploit-intel-inventory-leads.csv
exploit-intel-inventory-leads.json
sigma-rules\
```

`exploit-intel.json` enriches prioritized findings.

`exploit-intel-inventory-leads.csv` scans the full inventory for affected-driver filename mappings from the supplied research database. These are **research leads only**; the file explicitly states that affected version/build applicability is not established.

The main HTML report shows:

- Exploit-intel match count
- Public PoC/reference count
- ResearchMaturityScore
- maximum research CVSS
- inventory research leads
- number of generated Sigma rules

```

`Docs/SECURITY.md`:

```md
# Security Hardening

## Agent

- Agentdateien nur aus vertrauenswürdiger Quelle verteilen.
- `C:\Program Files\DriverRiskScout` nur für Administratoren/SYSTEM schreibbar lassen.
- API-Key nicht in `config.json` speichern.
- API-Key über Machine Environment Variable oder vorhandenes Secrets-/RMM-System setzen.
- Device ACL Probe standardmäßig deaktiviert lassen.
- Keine automatische Remediation/Löschung verwundbarer Treiber aktivieren: Hardware-/Boot-Ausfälle sind möglich.

## Collector

- Nie direkt unverschlüsselt ins LAN/Internet veröffentlichen.
- TLS-Reverse-Proxy verwenden.
- Ingest-Key und Admin-Passwort trennen.
- Lange zufällige Geheimnisse verwenden.
- Dashboard zusätzlich über VPN, IP-Allowlist oder SSO schützen.
- `server-data` mit restriktiven Dateirechten betreiben.
- Collector nicht mit Domain-Admin- oder Endpoint-Admin-Zugangsdaten ausstatten.
- Regelmäßige SQLite-Backups.
- Reverse-Proxy-/Container-/OS-Patches aktuell halten.

## Datenschutz

Standardmäßig können folgende Informationen zentral landen:
- Hostname/OS/Build,
- Treiberpfade,
- Hashes,
- Hersteller/Version,
- PnP-Geräteinformationen,
- AV/EDR-Produktnamen,
- Security-Posture,
- Event-Metadaten,
- vollständiges Driver-Inventar.

Vor Kundeneinsatz Auftrags-/Datenschutzanforderungen und Aufbewahrungszeit definieren.

## Keine Exploitability-Garantie

Ein `Known`-Hash bestätigt die Übereinstimmung mit einer Threat-Intelligence-Quelle. Er beweist nicht, dass ein Exploit auf genau diesem OS-/Policy-Kontext erfolgreich wäre.

`PolicyAssessment` ist eine Bewertung:
- `Observed loaded`
- `Observed loaded recently`
- `Code Integrity event observed`
- `Likely constrained by policy`
- `Policy protections present`
- `Unknown`

Nur echte Telemetrie wird als beobachteter Zustand bezeichnet.


## Online Investigation privacy and token scope

`DRS_ONLINE_ENRICHMENT=true` sends driver filenames, SHA-256 values and CVE identifiers to public GitHub/NVD APIs.

Set it to `false` where this egress is not acceptable.

Use a minimally privileged `DRS_GITHUB_TOKEN`; never reuse an administrative GitHub token. DriverRiskScout displays GitHub research metadata only and does not clone, compile or execute PoC code.


## Research database trust boundary

`Agent/profiles/exploit-db.json` is untrusted research input.

DriverRiskScout:

- parses it as data,
- does not import PowerShell/code from it,
- does not execute URLs,
- does not download referenced PoCs,
- does not promote filename-only matches into confirmed vulnerability findings,
- preserves the source's `Verified` flag only as source metadata.

Exact hash evidence and primary vendor/platform evidence remain stronger signals.

```

`Docs/SERVER-CLIENT.md`:

```md
# Server-/Client-Betrieb

## Grundprinzip

Der Windows-PC ist der **Agent/Client**. Er führt den Scan lokal aus.

Der **Collector/Server**:
- startet keine Scans remote,
- besitzt keine Admin-Credentials der Clients,
- sammelt Ergebnisse,
- speichert Historie,
- bietet Dashboard/API,
- ermöglicht Fleet Driver Search,
- kann zentrale Wazuh-Events erzeugen.

## Kommunikationsrichtung

Nur:

```text
Client -> HTTPS -> Collector
```

Standard-Endpunkt:

```text
POST /api/v1/ingest
```

Header:

```text
X-DRS-API-Key
```

## Firewall

Clients benötigen TCP/443 zum Reverse Proxy des Collectors.

Der Collector selbst lauscht im Docker-Setup nur auf:

```text
127.0.0.1:8088
```

Der Reverse Proxy übernimmt TLS.

## Offline-Fähigkeit

Wenn ein Notebook/Client den Collector nicht erreicht:

```text
C:\ProgramData\DriverRiskScout\Spool\pending-*.json
```

Der nächste Lauf sendet diese Queue zuerst.

## Fleet Driver Search

Dashboard:

```text
https://drs.example.local/drivers
```

Suche nach:
- `IOMap64.sys`
- vollständigem SHA-256

API:

```text
GET /api/v1/drivers?name=IOMap64.sys
GET /api/v1/drivers?sha256=<hash>
```

Damit lässt sich z. B. nach einem neuen LOLDrivers-Hash zentral prüfen, welche Endpoints betroffen sind.

## Empfohlene Rollen

### Kleine Umgebung
- 1 Linux-/Docker-Host als Collector
- täglicher Agenttask auf Windows
- SQLite
- optional Wazuh auf demselben Host

### Größere Umgebung
- TLS Reverse Proxy
- Collector hinter interner Load-Balancing-/Proxy-Schicht
- später PostgreSQL statt SQLite
- Wazuh/SIEM separat
- API-Key-Rotation über RMM/Secrets Management

## Key Rotation

1. Neuen Ingest-Key auf Collector konfigurieren.
2. Neuen Key auf Clients verteilen.
3. Collector neu starten.
4. Test-Scan.
5. Alten Key verwerfen.

Die aktuelle v2-Implementierung verwendet einen aktiven Ingest-Key. Für überlappende Rotation kann später Multi-Key-Unterstützung ergänzt werden.

## Datenmenge

`IncludeFullInventoryInAgentPayload=true` überträgt das komplette Driver-Inventar und ermöglicht die Fleet Driver Search.

Wenn Bandbreite/Speicher wichtiger ist:

```json
"IncludeFullInventoryInAgentPayload": false
```

Findings, Protection und Security-Product-State werden weiterhin übertragen; die zentrale Vollinventarsuche ist dann für diesen Snapshot nicht verfügbar.

```

`Docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md`:

```md
# Troubleshooting

## `pwsh.exe` nicht gefunden

DriverRiskScout 2.0 benötigt PowerShell 7.x.

```powershell
$PSVersionTable.PSVersion
```

## Parser-Test

```powershell
.\Test-DriverRiskScout.ps1
```

Dieser Test prüft Hauptskripte, Module und JSON-Dateien mit dem echten PowerShell-Parser.

## LOLDrivers JSON `init/INIT`

Der Core verwendet unter PowerShell 7 `ConvertFrom-Json -AsHashtable`, damit case-distinct JSON-Keys unterstützt werden.

## Collector Upload schlägt fehl

Prüfen:

```powershell
$env:DRS_COLLECTOR_API_KEY
Invoke-RestMethod https://drs.example.local/api/v1/health
```

Spool:

```text
C:\ProgramData\DriverRiskScout\Spool
```

## TLS-Fehler

Zertifikatskette/DNS korrigieren. `SkipCertificateCheck=true` nur zur kurzfristigen Diagnose.

## Keine SecurityCenter2-Daten

Auf Windows Server ist `root\SecurityCenter2` häufig nicht wie auf Windows-Clients verfügbar. DriverRiskScout nutzt deshalb zusätzlich Dienste, Prozesse, Softwareinventar und Defender-Cmdlets.

## CiTool nicht verfügbar

CiTool steht nicht auf allen älteren Windows-Versionen zur Verfügung. DriverRiskScout fällt auf aktive `.cip`-Dateien zurück; deren Policy-Eigenschaften sind dann teilweise `Unknown`.

## Wazuh empfängt nichts

Agent:

```powershell
Get-Content C:\ProgramData\DriverRiskScout\wazuh-driver-findings.ndjson
```

Wazuh-Agent-Konfiguration und Dienst prüfen.

Manager:

```bash
/var/ossec/bin/wazuh-logtest
```

## Exitcode 10 trotz erfolgreichem Scan

Exitcode 10 bedeutet: Scan erfolgreich, mindestens ein Medium-Finding. Das ist kein Programmfehler.

```

`Docs/UPGRADE.md`:

```md
# Upgrade

## 1.x -> 2.0

2.0 verwendet neue Pfade und ein neues Report-Schema.

Empfohlen:
1. Alte Version sichern.
2. 2.0 parallel in Testordner entpacken.
3. `Test-DriverRiskScout.ps1`.
4. Einen lokalen Scan ausführen.
5. Collector testen.
6. Erst dann Scheduled Task installieren.

Die 1.1.5-Core-Logik ist im Unterordner `Agent/Core` als Ausgangsbasis enthalten und wird von 2.0 orchestriert.

## State übernehmen

Alte `baseline.json` kann als Referenz archiviert werden. 2.0 baut unter:

```text
C:\ProgramData\DriverRiskScout\State
```

eine neue persistente First-/Last-Seen-Historie auf.

## Server

Vor Upgrade:
- Docker Volume `driver-riskscout_drs_data` sichern.
- `docker compose down`
- Paket aktualisieren.
- `docker compose up -d --build`

Schemaänderungen innerhalb 2.x sollen migrationsfähig gehalten werden.

```

that there is no warranty for this free software.  For both users' and
authors' sake, the GPL requires that modified versions be marked as
changed, so that their problems will not be attributed erroneously to

  Some devices are designed to deny users access to install or run
modified versions of the software inside them, although the manufacturer

`README.md`:

```md
# DriverRiskScout

**DriverRiskScout** is a defensive Windows driver security assessment tool for identifying known vulnerable, suspicious, legacy, and potentially abusable kernel drivers.

It inventories Windows drivers and correlates them with multiple security intelligence sources, including **LOLDrivers**, the **Microsoft Vulnerable Driver Blocklist**, and additional customizable **BYOVD detection profiles**.

DriverRiskScout is deliberately designed as a **read-only assessment tool**.

It does **not** exploit drivers, send IOCTL requests, load drivers, terminate processes, disable security products, or modify Windows security controls.

---

## Why DriverRiskScout?

Kernel drivers operate at one of the highest privilege levels on Windows systems.

A legitimately signed but vulnerable driver can potentially be abused by an attacker to gain kernel-level capabilities. This technique is commonly referred to as:

**BYOVD – Bring Your Own Vulnerable Driver**

Attackers may use vulnerable drivers to bypass or interfere with security mechanisms such as:

* Endpoint Detection and Response
* Antivirus software
* process protections
* kernel protections
* security monitoring
* privileged access controls

DriverRiskScout helps administrators and security professionals identify potentially risky drivers **before they become useful to an attacker**.

---

## Features

DriverRiskScout currently provides:

* Windows driver inventory
* SHA256-based driver identification
* Detection of known vulnerable drivers
* Detection of drivers known to be abused in attacks
* Integration with **LOLDrivers**
* Comparison against the **Microsoft Vulnerable Driver Blocklist**
* Support for additional custom BYOVD detection profiles
* Detection of suspicious driver characteristics
* DriverStore analysis
* Detection of stale DriverStore packages
* Severity classification
* Baseline comparison
* Change detection between scans
* Detailed HTML reporting
* NDJSON output
* Wazuh / SIEM integration support
* Read-only security assessment

---

## Security Model

DriverRiskScout was intentionally developed to avoid active exploitation.

The tool does **not**:

* send IOCTL requests
* attempt driver exploitation
* load vulnerable drivers
* unload kernel drivers
* disable Microsoft Defender
* disable EDR products
* terminate security processes
* modify the Windows registry for exploitation purposes
* change kernel security settings
* attempt privilege escalation

The assessment is based primarily on:

* driver metadata
* file hashes
* filesystem information
* installed driver information
* DriverStore information
* known vulnerable driver intelligence
* detection profiles

This makes DriverRiskScout suitable for defensive security assessments where actively interacting with kernel drivers would be inappropriate.

---

## Example

A typical DriverRiskScout scan may look similar to:

```text
=== DriverRiskScout 1.1.5 ===
Read-only assessment. No IOCTLs, exploitation, process termination,
driver loading or security-control changes are performed.

PowerShell runtime: 7.6.4

=== Threat intelligence ===
LOLDrivers entries loaded: 660
Microsoft blocklist source: Local Windows example policy
Exact hashes: 822
Bundled/custom BYOVD detection profiles: 5

=== Summary ===
Files inventoried: 909
Known findings:    1
Suspicious:        0
Critical/High:     0
Threat profiles:   0 match(es)
Stale DriverStore: 19
Baseline changes:  0
```

The exact number of detected drivers and threat-intelligence entries depends on the analyzed system and the available detection data.

---

## Requirements

DriverRiskScout is intended for Windows environments.

Recommended:

* Windows
* PowerShell 7
* Administrator privileges for complete driver visibility

Some information may still be available without administrative permissions, but running the assessment with elevated privileges provides more complete results.

---

## Running DriverRiskScout

Open **PowerShell 7** as Administrator.

Navigate to the DriverRiskScout directory:

```powershell
cd C:\Tools\DriverRiskScout
```

Run the main DriverRiskScout script.

For example:

```powershell
.\DriverRiskScout.ps1
```

> The exact script name may differ depending on the release package.

---

## Output

DriverRiskScout can generate multiple output formats for different use cases.

### Console Output

The console provides a quick summary of:

* inventoried drivers
* known findings
* suspicious findings
* Critical / High findings
* threat-profile matches
* stale DriverStore packages
* baseline changes

---

## HTML Report

DriverRiskScout generates a human-readable HTML security report.

The report is intended for:

* administrators
* penetration testers
* security analysts
* SOC teams
* customer documentation
* security assessments

Example:

```text
DriverRiskScout-report.html
```

The report can be opened locally in a web browser.

---

## Wazuh / SIEM Integration

DriverRiskScout can generate findings in **NDJSON** format.

Example:

```text
C:\ProgramData\DriverRiskScout\wazuh-driver-findings.ndjson
```

NDJSON output makes it easier to integrate DriverRiskScout findings into platforms such as:

* Wazuh
* Elasticsearch
* OpenSearch
* SIEM platforms
* log-processing pipelines
* custom SOC tooling

This allows driver findings to be correlated with other endpoint-security events.

---

## LOLDrivers Integration

DriverRiskScout can use intelligence from the **LOLDrivers** project.

LOLDrivers maintains information about legitimate Windows drivers that are vulnerable or have been observed being abused by attackers.

DriverRiskScout can correlate discovered driver hashes and metadata with this intelligence to identify known risks.

---

## Microsoft Vulnerable Driver Blocklist

Microsoft maintains a blocklist containing drivers considered vulnerable or potentially dangerous.

DriverRiskScout can compare discovered drivers against information derived from the Microsoft vulnerable driver blocklist.

This provides an additional detection source alongside community threat intelligence.

---

## BYOVD Detection Profiles

DriverRiskScout supports bundled and customizable detection profiles for identifying drivers associated with **Bring Your Own Vulnerable Driver** techniques.

Detection profiles can be used to describe known driver families or indicators beyond simple filename matching.

This allows DriverRiskScout to be extended as new vulnerable-driver techniques become known.

---

## DriverStore Analysis

Old driver packages may remain inside the Windows DriverStore even when they are no longer actively required.

DriverRiskScout can identify potentially stale DriverStore entries.

This is useful for:

* attack-surface reduction
* legacy-driver discovery
* system cleanup investigations
* identifying forgotten driver packages

A stale DriverStore entry is not automatically a security vulnerability.

It should be reviewed before removal.

---

## Baseline Monitoring

DriverRiskScout can compare the current driver state against a previous baseline.

This can help identify:

* newly installed drivers
* removed drivers
* changed driver files
* unexpected driver changes

Baseline monitoring can therefore complement normal vulnerability scanning.

---

## Severity

Findings may be classified according to their potential security relevance.

Examples include:

```text
Critical
High
Medium
Low
Informational
```

A detection does not automatically mean that a system has been compromised.

Findings should be reviewed together with:

* driver version
* hash
* system context
* driver usage
* vendor information
* Microsoft guidance
* threat intelligence

---

## Typical Use Cases

DriverRiskScout can be useful for:

### Windows Security Audits

Identify potentially vulnerable kernel drivers during security reviews.

### Penetration Testing Preparation

Assess whether known BYOVD-capable drivers already exist on a target system without actively exploiting them.

### Vulnerability Management

Add kernel-driver visibility to an existing vulnerability-management process.

### SOC Investigations

Check whether potentially abusable drivers exist during endpoint investigations.

### Incident Response

Identify drivers that may be relevant during forensic or security analysis.

### System Hardening

Find legacy or unnecessary drivers that increase the Windows kernel attack surface.

### Detection Engineering

Export findings to Wazuh or other SIEM platforms for further correlation.

---

## BYOVD

**Bring Your Own Vulnerable Driver** is a technique where an attacker introduces or abuses a legitimately signed but vulnerable kernel driver.

Because kernel drivers execute with extremely high privileges, a vulnerable driver may expose dangerous functionality to user-mode applications.

Depending on the vulnerability, this may allow attackers to perform actions such as:

* arbitrary kernel memory access
* process manipulation
* disabling security software
* privilege escalation
* kernel code execution

DriverRiskScout does **not** attempt any of these techniques.

It only attempts to identify potentially relevant drivers.

---

## False Positives

Not every detected driver represents an immediate vulnerability.

A finding can depend on factors such as:

* driver version
* operating system version
* configuration
* whether the driver is loaded
* whether a vulnerable interface is accessible
* vendor patches
* Microsoft mitigations

DriverRiskScout should therefore be used as an **assessment and investigation tool**, not as an automatic decision engine.

---

## Recommended Workflow

A typical assessment workflow could be:

```text
1. Run DriverRiskScout
        ↓
2. Review Critical / High findings
        ↓
3. Verify driver hash and version
        ↓
4. Check vendor and Microsoft information
        ↓
5. Determine whether the driver is required
        ↓
6. Update or remove unnecessary vulnerable drivers
        ↓
7. Run DriverRiskScout again
        ↓
8. Store the result as a new baseline
```

---

## Defensive Use

DriverRiskScout is developed for:

* defensive security
* authorized penetration testing
* vulnerability assessment
* system administration
* incident response
* security research
* educational purposes

Only analyze systems that you own or have explicit authorization to assess.

---

## Disclaimer

DriverRiskScout is provided for security assessment and research purposes.

The detection of a driver does not necessarily mean that the driver is exploitable on the analyzed system.

Likewise, the absence of a finding does not guarantee that a system is secure.

Always validate findings independently before making changes to production systems.

The author assumes no responsibility for damage, data loss, system outages, or other consequences resulting from the use of this software.

---

## Project Philosophy

DriverRiskScout follows a simple principle:

> **Detect dangerous kernel exposure without becoming an exploitation tool itself.**

The focus is on visibility, threat intelligence, and defensive assessment.

## Final Note

Windows driver security is often overlooked during traditional vulnerability assessments.

DriverRiskScout aims to make this part of the Windows attack surface more visible.

**Know which kernel drivers are present before an attacker finds a way to abuse them.**

`Server/Caddyfile.example`:

```example
# Example only. Replace hostname and certificate strategy.

    reverse_proxy 127.0.0.1:8088
    request_body {
        max_size 30MB
